Well… I guess what finally got me to come in is this thing that’s been happening with eating, mostly after my evening class. I’ll go in thinking I’m just going to grab something quick because I haven’t really eaten much or I feel like I’m catching up from the day, and then it turns into more than I meant.

Like, I might stop for a full fast-food meal, and then I’ll get candy from a vending machine, and then even after that, when I get home, I’ll end up eating more snacks. It feels like I’m making up for the whole day all at once, if that makes sense. It’s not every second of my life, but it’s enough that I’m worried about it and embarrassed, and I thought maybe I should talk to someone before it gets worse.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… that’s the part that scares me a little, honestly. It doesn’t feel like normal snacking once it starts. I’ll tell myself, “Okay, just have a little and then put it away,” or “Close the bag and go to bed, you’re done,” and I’ll even sort of mean it in that moment.

But then my hand just keeps going back in. Like I’m watching myself do it and arguing with myself at the same time. I’ll think, “Why are you still eating this?” and then I’m still reaching for more anyway. It keeps going until I feel embarrassed and kind of sick of myself, like I should’ve been able to stop and I didn’t. So… no, I guess I don’t feel very in control during those times.

I mean… I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I don’t know if “guilty” is exactly the word every time. Sometimes I mostly feel embarrassed, like I don’t want anyone to know how much I ate or how fast it happened, and I’ll kind of replay it in my head afterward.

I’m not really sure, honestly. I mean, I notice little ups and downs, like my pants feeling tighter some weeks and then maybe normal again, but I haven’t been tracking it in any serious way. I don’t weigh myself consistently, partly because I don’t really want to know, if I’m being honest.

Yeah… I have. I don’t really like saying that out loud, but yes. Not every time, and I wouldn’t say it’s something I’ve done constantly, but a few times after those late-night eating spells, I’ve gone to the bathroom and made myself throw up.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It’s hard for me to put an exact number on the ones where I don’t throw up afterward, because I don’t really keep track in a neat way. But it does happen sometimes after my evening class.

What usually happens is I’ll leave class feeling like I’ve been holding myself together all day, and then I’ll stop for food — like a full fast-food meal, not just a snack — and then I might get candy from a vending machine, and when I get home I’ll still keep going with whatever snacks are there. It feels almost like I’m making up for the whole day, like I didn’t eat “normally” earlier or I was trying to be careful, and then once I start, it just snowballs.

Sometimes after that I do make myself throw up, like I said, but other times I just… don’t. I sit with it, feel awful and overly full and embarrassed, and tell myself I shouldn’t do it again. But the eating a lot after class part is really the main pattern that brought me in.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I guess what finally got me to come in is this thing that’s been happening with eating, mostly after my evening class. I’ll go in thinking I’m just going to grab something quick because I haven’t really eaten much or I feel like I’m catching up from the day, and then it turns into more than I meant.\n\nLike, I might stop for a full fast-food meal, and then I’ll get candy from a vending machine, and then even after that, when I get home, I’ll end up eating more snacks. It feels like I’m making up for the whole day all at once, if that makes sense. It’s not every second of my life, but it’s enough that I’m worried about it and embarrassed, and I thought maybe I should talk to someone before it gets worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these episodes after your evening class?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s the part that scares me a little, honestly. It doesn’t feel like normal snacking once it starts. Like I’m watching myself do it and arguing with myself at the same time. I’ll think, “Why are you still eating this?” and then I’m still reaching for more anyway. It keeps going until I feel embarrassed and kind of sick of myself, like I should’ve been able to stop and I didn’t. It’s not like I’ve sat there and put a name to it, if that makes sense. I just know I don’t feel good about it afterward.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over time due to these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I know that probably sounds… I don’t know, not completely logical, but in the moment I get scared and kind of panicky about it.\n\nSo I’ve done that to try to undo it, I guess. But if I’m being honest, it has been pretty regular. Usually that one night a week where it happens, and it’s been going on for a few months now.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up afterward?\n\nPatient: It’s hard for me to put an exact number on the ones where I don’t throw up afterward, because I don’t really keep track in a neat way. But it does happen sometimes after my evening class.\n\nWhat usually happens is I’ll leave class feeling like I’ve been holding myself together all day, and then I’ll stop for food — like a full fast-food meal, not just a snack — and then I might get candy from a vending machine, and when I get home I’ll still keep going with whatever snacks are there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’d say maybe a few times a month… probably around two or three times where I eat a lot after class and don’t make myself throw up afterward.

It’s that pattern where I leave my evening class and I feel like I’ve been trying to be “good” all day, or holding off on eating much, and then I stop and get a full fast-food meal. And then sometimes it’s like that doesn’t end it — I’ll get candy from a vending machine, and when I get home I’ll start in on snacks there too. It feels like I’m making up for the whole day all at once, if that makes sense.

Some of those times I do end up throwing up afterward, but the ones where I don’t… maybe two or three in a month. I’m not tracking it on a calendar, so I’m a little embarrassed guessing, but that sounds about right.

I’m not totally sure how to separate those, honestly. I mean… I definitely feel bad afterward, but whether I’d call it guilt versus embarrassment or frustration, I don’t know.

A lot of the time it’s more like this heavy feeling of, “Why did I do that again?” and I feel kind of ashamed sitting there thinking about it. It’s more tangled than that — embarrassed, annoyed at myself, disappointed, and wanting to pretend it didn’t happen.

So… yes, there’s some guilt mixed in maybe, but I’m not sure I can cleanly say it’s guilt instead of the other feelings.

failed at being the careful, disciplined person I try to be, even if the rest of my bookkeeping work is fine.\n- key: S048\n  name: Compensatory_Behavior_Purging\n  description: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.\n  manifestation: A few times after those late-night eating spells, I have gone to the bathroom and made myself throw up because I was scared all that food would show on me.\n- key: S049\n  name: Eating_Large_Amount\n  description: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is classes.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 55 / bookkeeping_accounting_or_auditing_clerk / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S049\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up afterward?\n\nPatient: It’s hard for me to put an exact number on the ones where I don’t throw up afterward, because I don’t really keep track in a neat way. But it does happen sometimes after my evening class.\n\nWhat usually happens is I’ll leave class feeling like I’ve been holding myself together all day, and then I’ll stop for food — like a full fast-food meal, not just a snack — and then I might get candy from a vending machine, and when I get home I’ll still keep going with whatever snacks are there. It feels almost like I’m making up for the whole day, like I didn’t eat “normally” earlier or I was trying to be careful, and then once I start, it just snowballs.\n\nSometimes after that I do make myself throw up, like I said, but other times I just… don’t. And then sometimes it’s like that doesn’t end it — I’ll get candy from a vending machine, and when I get home I’ll start in on snacks there too. It feels like I’m making up for the whole day all at once, if that makes sense.\n\nSome of those times I do end up throwing up afterward, but the ones where I don’t… maybe two or three in a month. I’m not tracking it on a calendar, so I’m a little embarrassed guessing, but that sounds about right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after these episodes of eating are over, rather than just embarrassed or frustrated?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to separate those, honestly. But I don’t always have a clear thought like I did something morally wrong, if that’s what you mean by guilty. It’s more tangled than that — embarrassed, annoyed at myself, disappointed, and wanting to pretend it didn’t happen.\n\nSo… yes, there’s some guilt mixed in maybe, but I’m not sure I can cleanly say it’s guilt instead of the other feelings.\n\n[Last Question]\nHow many times a week do you have episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up afterward?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
In a week, I guess it’s not every week in the exact same way. If I’m trying to average it out, the times where I eat a lot after class and don’t make myself throw up afterward are maybe… less than once a week? Like some weeks it doesn’t happen, and then another week it might happen once.

But the pattern is pretty specific when it does happen. After my evening class, I’ll feel like I’ve been holding back all day, and then I stop and get a full fast-food meal, and sometimes I’ll add candy from a vending machine, and then when I get home I still keep eating snacks. It feels like I’m trying to make up for the whole day all at once.

So if you want it in “per week” terms, I’d probably say around once a week at most, and some weeks not at all.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I guess what finally got me to come in is this thing that’s been happening with eating, mostly after my evening class. I’ll go in thinking I’m just going to grab something quick because I haven’t really eaten much or I feel like I’m catching up from the day, and then it turns into more than I meant.\n\nLike, I might stop for a full fast-food meal, and then I’ll get candy from a vending machine, and then even after that, when I get home, I’ll end up eating more snacks. It feels like I’m making up for the whole day all at once, if that makes sense. It’s not every second of my life, but it’s enough that I’m worried about it and embarrassed, and I thought maybe I should talk to someone before it gets worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these episodes after your evening class?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s the part that scares me a little, honestly. It doesn’t feel like normal snacking once it starts. I’ll tell myself, “Okay, just have a little and then put it away,” or “Close the bag and go to bed, you’re done,” and I’ll even sort of mean it in that moment.\n\nBut then my hand just keeps going back in. Like I’m watching myself do it and arguing with myself at the same time. I’ll think, “Why are you still eating this?” and then I’m still reaching for more anyway. It keeps going until I feel embarrassed and kind of sick of myself, like I should’ve been able to stop and I didn’t. So… no, I guess I don’t feel very in control during those times.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after these episodes of eating are over?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I don’t know if “guilty” is exactly the word every time. Sometimes I mostly feel embarrassed, like I don’t want anyone to know how much I ate or how fast it happened, and I’ll kind of replay it in my head afterward.\n\nThere’s definitely this feeling of, “Why did I do that again?” or “I should have had more self-control,” but I don’t know if that counts as guilt or just being frustrated with myself. It’s not like I’ve sat there and put a name to it, if that makes sense. I just know I don’t feel good about it afterward.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over time due to these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I mean, I notice little ups and downs, like my pants feeling tighter some weeks and then maybe normal again, but I haven’t been tracking it in any serious way. I don’t weigh myself consistently, partly because I don’t really want to know, if I’m being honest.\n\nSo I can’t say there’s been some big, clear change where I know, “Oh, this is because of those nights after class.” It’s more that I worry it might be affecting me, and then I try not to think about it too much. I haven’t noticed anything dramatic enough that I could point to it for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for these episodes of eating by doing something to counteract the calories you consumed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I don’t really like saying that out loud, but yes. Not every time, and I wouldn’t say it’s something I’ve done constantly, but a few times after those late-night eating spells, I’ve gone to the bathroom and made myself throw up.\n\nIt’s usually after I’ve eaten way more than I meant to, and then I start thinking about it sitting in me, like all that food is going to immediately show on my body or make my clothes tighter or something. I know that probably sounds… I don’t know, not completely logical, but in the moment I get scared and kind of panicky about it.\n\nSo I’ve done that to try to undo it, I guess. Or make it feel like it didn’t count as much. And afterward I don’t exactly feel proud or relieved for very long. Mostly I feel ashamed that I did that too.\n\nDoctor: How often have you made yourself throw up after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: For about the last three months, I’d say it’s been… usually once a week. Like, there’s typically at least one night each week after class where I end up overeating like that, and then afterward I make myself throw up.\n\nIt’s not like every single day or after every meal or anything like that, and I think part of me wants to make it sound less serious because of that. But if I’m being honest, it has been pretty regular. Usually that one night a week where it happens, and it’s been going on for a few months now.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up afterward?\n\nPatient: It’s hard for me to put an exact number on the ones where I don’t throw up afterward, because I don’t really keep track in a neat way. But it does happen sometimes after my evening class.\n\nWhat usually happens is I’ll leave class feeling like I’ve been holding myself together all day, and then I’ll stop for food — like a full fast-food meal, not just a snack — and then I might get candy from a vending machine, and when I get home I’ll still keep going with whatever snacks are there. It feels almost like I’m making up for the whole day, like I didn’t eat “normally” earlier or I was trying to be careful, and then once I start, it just snowballs.\n\nSometimes after that I do make myself throw up, like I said, but other times I just… don’t. For the ones where I do make myself throw up… that’s more regular, unfortunately. I’d say for about the last three months, there’s usually been at least one night each week after class where I overeat like that and then make myself throw up afterward.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I guess what finally got me to come in is this thing that’s been happening with eating, mostly after my evening class. I’ll go in thinking I’m just going to grab something quick because I haven’t really eaten much or I feel like I’m catching up from the day, and then it turns into more than I meant.\n\nLike, I might stop for a full fast-food meal, and then I’ll get candy from a vending machine, and then even after that, when I get home, I’ll end up eating more snacks. It feels like I’m making up for the whole day all at once, if that makes sense. It’s not every second of my life, but it’s enough that I’m worried about it and embarrassed, and I thought maybe I should talk to someone before it gets worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these episodes after your evening class?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s the part that scares me a little, honestly. It doesn’t feel like normal snacking once it starts. I’ll tell myself, “Okay, just have a little and then put it away,” or “Close the bag and go to bed, you’re done,” and I’ll even sort of mean it in that moment.\n\nBut then my hand just keeps going back in. Like I’m watching myself do it and arguing with myself at the same time. I’ll think, “Why are you still eating this?” and then I’m still reaching for more anyway. It keeps going until I feel embarrassed and kind of sick of myself, like I should’ve been able to stop and I didn’t. So… no, I guess I don’t feel very in control during those times.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after these episodes of eating are over?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I don’t know if “guilty” is exactly the word every time. Sometimes I mostly feel embarrassed, like I don’t want anyone to know how much I ate or how fast it happened, and I’ll kind of replay it in my head afterward.\n\nThere’s definitely this feeling of, “Why did I do that again?” or “I should have had more self-control,” but I don’t know if that counts as guilt or just being frustrated with myself. It’s not like I’ve sat there and put a name to it, if that makes sense. I just know I don’t feel good about it afterward.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over time due to these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I mean, I notice little ups and downs, like my pants feeling tighter some weeks and then maybe normal again, but I haven’t been tracking it in any serious way. I don’t weigh myself consistently, partly because I don’t really want to know, if I’m being honest.\n\nSo I can’t say there’s been some big, clear change where I know, “Oh, this is because of those nights after class.” It’s more that I worry it might be affecting me, and then I try not to think about it too much. I haven’t noticed anything dramatic enough that I could point to it for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for these episodes of eating by doing something to counteract the calories you consumed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I don’t really like saying that out loud, but yes. Not every time, and I wouldn’t say it’s something I’ve done constantly, but a few times after those late-night eating spells, I’ve gone to the bathroom and made myself throw up.\n\nIt’s usually after I’ve eaten way more than I meant to, and then I start thinking about it sitting in me, like all that food is going to immediately show on my body or make my clothes tighter or something. I know that probably sounds… I don’t know, not completely logical, but in the moment I get scared and kind of panicky about it.\n\nSo I’ve done that to try to undo it, I guess. Or make it feel like it didn’t count as much. And afterward I don’t exactly feel proud or relieved for very long. Mostly I feel ashamed that I did that too.\n\nDoctor: How often have you made yourself throw up after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: For about the last three months, I’d say it’s been… usually once a week. Like, there’s typically at least one night each week after class where I end up overeating like that, and then afterward I make myself throw up.\n\nIt’s not like every single day or after every meal or anything like that, and I think part of me wants to make it sound less serious because of that. But if I’m being honest, it has been pretty regular. Usually that one night a week where it happens, and it’s been going on for a few months now.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up afterward?\n\nPatient: It’s hard for me to put an exact number on the ones where I don’t throw up afterward, because I don’t really keep track in a neat way. But it does happen sometimes after my evening class.\n\nWhat usually happens is I’ll leave class feeling like I’ve been holding myself together all day, and then I’ll stop for food — like a full fast-food meal, not just a snack — and then I might get candy from a vending machine, and when I get home I’ll still keep going with whatever snacks are there. It feels almost like I’m making up for the whole day, like I didn’t eat “normally” earlier or I was trying to be careful, and then once I start, it just snowballs.\n\nSometimes after that I do make myself throw up, like I said, but other times I just… don’t. I sit with it, feel awful and overly full and embarrassed, and tell myself I shouldn’t do it again. But the eating a lot after class part is really the main pattern that brought me in.\n\nDoctor: How many times a month would you say you have these episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up afterward?\n\nPatient: I’d say maybe a few times a month… probably around two or three times where I eat a lot after class and don’t make myself throw up afterward.\n\nIt’s that pattern where I leave my evening class and I feel like I’ve been trying to be “good” all day, or holding off on eating much, and then I stop and get a full fast-food meal. I know that probably sounds… I don’t know, not completely logical, but in the moment I get scared and kind of panicky about it.\n\nSo I’ve done that to try to undo it, I guess. Or make it feel like it didn’t count as much. And afterward I don’t exactly feel proud or relieved for very long. Mostly I feel ashamed that I did that too.\n\nDoctor: How often have you made yourself throw up after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: For about the last three months, I’d say it’s been… usually once a week. Like, there’s typically at least one night each week after class where I end up overeating like that, and then afterward I make myself throw up.\n\nIt’s not like every single day or after every meal or anything like that, and I think part of me wants to make it sound less serious because of that. But if I’m being honest, it has been pretty regular. Usually that one night a week where it happens, and it’s been going on for a few months now.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up afterward?\n\nPatient: It’s hard for me to put an exact number on the ones where I don’t throw up afterward, because I don’t really keep track in a neat way. But it does happen sometimes after my evening class.\n\nWhat usually happens is I’ll leave class feeling like I’ve been holding myself together all day, and then I’ll stop for food — like a full fast-food meal, not just a snack — and then I might get candy from a vending machine, and when I get home I’ll still keep going with whatever snacks are there. It feels almost like I’m making up for the whole day, like I didn’t eat “normally” earlier or I was trying to be careful, and then once I start, it just snowballs.\n\nSometimes after that I do make myself throw up, like I said, but other times I just… don’t. I sit with it, feel awful and overly full and embarrassed, and tell myself I shouldn’t do it again. But the eating a lot after class part is really the main pattern that brought me in.\n\nDoctor: How many times a month would you say you have these episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up afterward?\n\nPatient: I’d say maybe a few times a month… probably around two or three times where I eat a lot after class and don’t make myself throw up afterward.\n\nIt’s that pattern where I leave my evening class and I feel like I’ve been trying to be “good” all day, or holding off on eating much, and then I stop and get a full fast-food meal. And then sometimes it’s like that doesn’t end it — I’ll get candy from a vending machine, and when I get home I’ll start in on snacks there too. It feels like I’m making up for the whole day all at once, if that makes sense.\n\nSome of those times I do end up throwing up afterward, but the ones where I don’t… maybe two or three in a month. I’m not tracking it on a calendar, so I’m a little embarrassed guessing, but that sounds about right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after these episodes of eating are over, rather than just embarrassed or frustrated?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to separate those, honestly. I mean… I definitely feel bad afterward, but whether I’d call it guilt versus embarrassment or frustration, I don’t know.\n\nA lot of the time it’s more like this heavy feeling of, “Why did I do that again?” and I feel kind of ashamed sitting there thinking about it. But I don’t always have a clear thought like I did something morally wrong, if that’s what you mean by guilty. It’s more tangled than that — embarrassed, annoyed at myself, disappointed, and wanting to pretend it didn’t happen.\n\nSo… yes, there’s some guilt mixed in maybe, but I’m not sure I can cleanly say it’s guilt instead of the other feelings.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you have episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up afterward?\n\nPatient: In a week, I guess it’s not every week in the exact same way. If I’m trying to average it out, the times where I eat a lot after class and don’t make myself throw up afterward are maybe… less than once a week? Like some weeks it doesn’t happen, and then another week it might happen once.\n\nBut the pattern is pretty specific when it does happen. After my evening class, I’ll feel like I’ve been holding back all day, and then I stop and get a full fast-food meal, and sometimes I’ll add candy from a vending machine, and then when I get home I still keep eating snacks. It feels like I’m trying to make up for the whole day all at once.\n\nSo if you want it in “per week” terms, I’d probably say around once a week at most, and some weeks not at all.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes where you eat a lot and then make yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: For the ones where I do make myself throw up… that’s more regular, unfortunately. I’d say for about the last three months, there’s usually been at least one night each week after class where I overeat like that and then make myself throw up afterward.\n\nIt’s not like I’m writing it down, so I’m trying not to overstate it, but when I think back week by week, that’s about right — usually once a week, sometimes it feels like that one night after class where it happens. But the eating a lot after class part is really the main pattern that brought me in.\n\nDoctor: How many times a month would you say you have these episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up afterward?\n\nPatient: I’d say maybe a few times a month… probably around two or three times where I eat a lot after class and don’t make myself throw up afterward.\n\nIt’s that pattern where I leave my evening class and I feel like I’ve been trying to be “good” all day, or holding off on eating much, and then I stop and get a full fast-food meal. It feels like I’m making up for the whole day all at once, if that makes sense.\n\nSome of those times I do end up throwing up afterward, but the ones where I don’t… maybe two or three in a month. I’m not tracking it on a calendar, so I’m a little embarrassed guessing, but that sounds about right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after these episodes of eating are over, rather than just embarrassed or frustrated?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to separate those, honestly. I mean… I definitely feel bad afterward, but whether I’d call it guilt versus embarrassment or frustration, I don’t know.\n\nA lot of the time it’s more like this heavy feeling of, “Why did I do that again?” and I feel kind of ashamed sitting there thinking about it. But I don’t always have a clear thought like I did something morally wrong, if that’s what you mean by guilty. It’s more tangled than that — embarrassed, annoyed at myself, disappointed, and wanting to pretend it didn’t happen.\n\nSo… yes, there’s some guilt mixed in maybe, but I’m not sure I can cleanly say it’s guilt instead of the other feelings.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you have episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up afterward?\n\nPatient: In a week, I guess it’s not every week in the exact same way. If I’m trying to average it out, the times where I eat a lot after class and don’t make myself throw up afterward are maybe… less than once a week? Like some weeks it doesn’t happen, and then another week it might happen once.\n\nBut the pattern is pretty specific when it does happen. After my evening class, I’ll feel like I’ve been holding back all day, and then I stop and get a full fast-food meal, and sometimes I’ll add candy from a vending machine, and then when I get home I still keep eating snacks. It feels like I’m trying to make up for the whole day all at once.\n\nSo if you want it in “per week” terms, I’d probably say around once a week at most, and some weeks not at all.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes where you eat a lot and then make yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: For the ones where I do make myself throw up… that’s more regular, unfortunately. I’d say for about the last three months, there’s usually been at least one night each week after class where I overeat like that and then make myself throw up afterward.\n\nIt’s not like I’m writing it down, so I’m trying not to overstate it, but when I think back week by week, that’s about right — usually once a week, sometimes it feels like that one night after class where it happens. I’ll eat more than I meant to, and then afterward I panic or feel awful about it, and I end up making myself throw up.\n\nDoctor: How many episodes of making yourself throw up after eating have you had in the past month?\n\nPatient: In the past month… I think it’s been a few times. I’m trying to count it without making it sound more organized than it is, because it’s not like I mark it down or anything.\n\nIt happens after those late-night eating spells, especially when I feel like I’ve eaten way too much and then I start worrying that all that food is going to show on me somehow. A few times I’ve gone into the bathroom afterward and made myself throw up. It’s embarrassing to say out loud, but yes — in the past month, I’d say a few episodes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have more episodes of eating a lot after class where you don't make yourself throw up, or more episodes where you do make yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: More of the ones where I do make myself throw up, I think. I’ll go in thinking I’m just going to grab something quick because I haven’t really eaten much or I feel like I’m catching up from the day, and then it turns into more than I meant.\n\nLike, I might stop for a full fast-food meal, and then I’ll get candy from a vending machine, and then even after that, when I get home, I’ll end up eating more snacks. It feels like I’m making up for the whole day all at once, if that makes sense. It’s not every second of my life, but it’s enough that I’m worried about it and embarrassed, and I thought maybe I should talk to someone before it gets worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these episodes after your evening class?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s the part that scares me a little, honestly. It doesn’t feel like normal snacking once it starts. I’ll tell myself, “Okay, just have a little and then put it away,” or “Close the bag and go to bed, you’re done,” and I’ll even sort of mean it in that moment.\n\nBut then my hand just keeps going back in. Like I’m watching myself do it and arguing with myself at the same time. I’ll think, “Why are you still eating this?” and then I’m still reaching for more anyway. It keeps going until I feel embarrassed and kind of sick of myself, like I should’ve been able to stop and I didn’t. So… no, I guess I don’t feel very in control during those times.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after these episodes of eating are over?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I don’t know if “guilty” is exactly the word every time. Sometimes I mostly feel embarrassed, like I don’t want anyone to know how much I ate or how fast it happened, and I’ll kind of replay it in my head afterward.\n\nThere’s definitely this feeling of, “Why did I do that again?” or “I should have had more self-control,” but I don’t know if that counts as guilt or just being frustrated with myself. It’s not like I’ve sat there and put a name to it, if that makes sense. I just know I don’t feel good about it afterward.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over time due to these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I mean, I notice little ups and downs, like my pants feeling tighter some weeks and then maybe normal again, but I haven’t been tracking it in any serious way. I don’t weigh myself consistently, partly because I don’t really want to know, if I’m being honest.\n\nSo I can’t say there’s been some big, clear change where I know, “Oh, this is because of those nights after class.” It’s more that I worry it might be affecting me, and then I try not to think about it too much. I haven’t noticed anything dramatic enough that I could point to it for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for these episodes of eating by doing something to counteract the calories you consumed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I don’t really like saying that out loud, but yes. Not every time, and I wouldn’t say it’s something I’ve done constantly, but a few times after those late-night eating spells, I’ve gone to the bathroom and made myself throw up.\n\nIt’s usually after I’ve eaten way more than I meant to, and then I start thinking about it sitting in me, like all that food is going to immediately show on my body or make my clothes tighter or something. I know that probably sounds… I don’t know, not completely logical, but in the moment I get scared and kind of panicky about it.\n\nSo I’ve done that to try to undo it, I guess. Or make it feel like it didn’t count as much. And afterward I don’t exactly feel proud or relieved for very long. Mostly I feel ashamed that I did that too.\n\nDoctor: How often have you made yourself throw up after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: For about the last three months, I’d say it’s been… usually once a week. Like, there’s typically at least one night each week after class where I end up overeating like that, and then afterward I make myself throw up.\n\nIt’s not like every single day or after every meal or anything like that, and I think part of me wants to make it sound less serious because of that. But if I’m being honest, it has been pretty regular. Usually that one night a week where it happens, and it’s been going on for a few months now.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up afterward?\n\nPatient: It’s hard for me to put an exact number on the ones where I don’t throw up afterward, because I don’t really keep track in a neat way. But it does happen sometimes after my evening class.\n\nWhat usually happens is I’ll leave class feeling like I’ve been holding myself together all day, and then I’ll stop for food — like a full fast-food meal, not just a snack — and then I might get candy from a vending machine, and when I get home I’ll still keep going with whatever snacks are there. It feels almost like I’m making up for the whole day, like I didn’t eat “normally” earlier or I was trying to be careful, and then once I start, it just snowballs.\n\nSometimes after that I do make myself throw up, like I said, but other times I just… don’t. I sit with it, feel awful and overly full and embarrassed, and tell myself I shouldn’t do it again. But the eating a lot after class part is really the main pattern that brought me in.\n\nDoctor: How many times a month would you say you have these episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up afterward?\n\nPatient: I’d say maybe a few times a month… probably around two or three times where I eat a lot after class and don’t make myself throw up afterward.\n\nIt’s that pattern where I leave my evening class and I feel like I’ve been trying to be “good” all day, or holding off on eating much, and then I stop and get a full fast-food meal. And then sometimes it’s like that doesn’t end it — I’ll get candy from a vending machine, and when I get home I’ll start in on snacks there too. It feels like I’m making up for the whole day all at once, if that makes sense.\n\nSome of those times I do end up throwing up afterward, but the ones where I don’t… maybe two or three in a month. I’m not tracking it on a calendar, so I’m a little embarrassed guessing, but that sounds about right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after these episodes of eating are over, rather than just embarrassed or frustrated?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to separate those, honestly. I mean… I definitely feel bad afterward, but whether I’d call it guilt versus embarrassment or frustration, I don’t know.\n\nA lot of the time it’s more like this heavy feeling of, “Why did I do that again?” and I feel kind of ashamed sitting there thinking about it. But I don’t always have a clear thought like I did something morally wrong, if that’s what you mean by guilty. It’s more tangled than that — embarrassed, annoyed at myself, disappointed, and wanting to pretend it didn’t happen.\n\nSo… yes, there’s some guilt mixed in maybe, but I’m not sure I can cleanly say it’s guilt instead of the other feelings.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you have episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up afterward?\n\nPatient: In a week, I guess it’s not every week in the exact same way. If I’m trying to average it out, the times where I eat a lot after class and don’t make myself throw up afterward are maybe… less than once a week? Like some weeks it doesn’t happen, and then another week it might happen once.\n\nBut the pattern is pretty specific when it does happen. After my evening class, I’ll feel like I’ve been holding back all day, and then I stop and get a full fast-food meal, and sometimes I’ll add candy from a vending machine, and then when I get home I still keep eating snacks. It feels like I’m trying to make up for the whole day all at once.\n\nSo if you want it in “per week” terms, I’d probably say around once a week at most, and some weeks not at all.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes where you eat a lot and then make yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: For the ones where I do make myself throw up… that’s more regular, unfortunately. I’d say for about the last three months, there’s usually been at least one night each week after class where I overeat like that and then make myself throw up afterward.\n\nIt’s not like I’m writing it down, so I’m trying not to overstate it, but when I think back week by week, that’s about right — usually once a week, sometimes it feels like that one night after class where it happens. I’ll eat more than I meant to, and then afterward I panic or feel awful about it, and I end up making myself throw up.\n\nDoctor: How many episodes of making yourself throw up after eating have you had in the past month?\n\nPatient: In the past month… I think it’s been a few times. I’m trying to count it without making it sound more organized than it is, because it’s not like I mark it down or anything.\n\nIt happens after those late-night eating spells, especially when I feel like I’ve eaten way too much and then I start worrying that all that food is going to show on me somehow. A few times I’ve gone into the bathroom afterward and made myself throw up. It’s embarrassing to say out loud, but yes — in the past month, I’d say a few episodes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have more episodes of eating a lot after class where you don't make yourself throw up, or more episodes where you do make yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: More of the ones where I do make myself throw up, I think. I mean, there are times after class where I eat too much and just… go to bed feeling terrible about it, but the ones that stick out are the nights where I get scared afterward.\n\nA few times after those late-night eating spells, I’ve gone to the bathroom and made myself throw up because I start thinking all that food is going to show on me somehow. It’s not every single time I overeat, but if you’re asking which happens more, I’d say the throwing up afterward has happened more than the times I just leave it alone.\n\nDoctor: On average, how many episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up do you have in a typical month?\n\nPatient: Maybe… a couple? It’s hard for me to separate it cleanly, because the whole thing usually starts the same way after my evening class. I’ll stop for food, and it’s not just like a normal snack — I’ll get a full fast-food meal, then candy from the vending machine, and then when I get home I’ll still keep eating, like chips or whatever snacks are around.\n\nThe times where I eat that much but don’t make myself throw up… I’d say maybe one or two times in a typical month. Most of the time, if it gets to that point where I’ve eaten like I’m making up for the whole day, I end up really upset afterward. But yes, there are some nights where I just go home, eat all that, feel awful, and don’t do anything after.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t think so… not in the way you mean, I don’t think.

Like, I don’t usually go exercise afterward to “make up for it.” I might feel really guilty and restless after eating like that, and I’ll think things like, “I should do something,” but I don’t really have a pattern where I go running or work out for hours after class because I ate too much.

I’m not much of an exercise person to begin with, honestly, and after those nights I’m usually more exhausted and ashamed than motivated. So if you mean specifically eating a lot after class and then doing a bunch of exercise to compensate, no, I haven’t really noticed that happening.

    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.2, F50.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I guess what finally got me to come in is this thing that’s been happening with eating, mostly after my evening class. I’ll go in thinking I’m just going to grab something quick because I haven’t really eaten much or I feel like I’m catching up from the day, and then it turns into more than I meant.\n\nLike, I might stop for a full fast-food meal, and then I’ll get candy from a vending machine, and then even after that, when I get home, I’ll end up eating more snacks. It feels like I’m making up for the whole day all at once, if that makes sense. It’s not every second of my life, but it’s enough that I’m worried about it and embarrassed, and I thought maybe I should talk to someone before it gets worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these episodes after your evening class?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s the part that scares me a little, honestly. It doesn’t feel like normal snacking once it starts. I’ll tell myself, “Okay, just have a little and then put it away,” or “Close the bag and go to bed, you’re done,” and I’ll even sort of mean it in that moment.\n\nBut then my hand just keeps going back in. Like I’m watching myself do it and arguing with myself at the same time. I’ll think, “Why are you still eating this?” and then I’m still reaching for more anyway. It keeps going until I feel embarrassed and kind of sick of myself, like I should’ve been able to stop and I didn’t. So… no, I guess I don’t feel very in control during those times.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after these episodes of eating are over?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I don’t know if “guilty” is exactly the word every time. Sometimes I mostly feel embarrassed, like I don’t want anyone to know how much I ate or how fast it happened, and I’ll kind of replay it in my head afterward.\n\nThere’s definitely this feeling of, “Why did I do that again?” or “I should have had more self-control,” but I don’t know if that counts as guilt or just being frustrated with myself. It’s not like I’ve sat there and put a name to it, if that makes sense. I just know I don’t feel good about it afterward.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over time due to these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I mean, I notice little ups and downs, like my pants feeling tighter some weeks and then maybe normal again, but I haven’t been tracking it in any serious way. I don’t weigh myself consistently, partly because I don’t really want to know, if I’m being honest.\n\nSo I can’t say there’s been some big, clear change where I know, “Oh, this is because of those nights after class.” It’s more that I worry it might be affecting me, and then I try not to think about it too much. I haven’t noticed anything dramatic enough that I could point to it for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for these episodes of eating by doing something to counteract the calories you consumed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I don’t really like saying that out loud, but yes. Not every time, and I wouldn’t say it’s something I’ve done constantly, but a few times after those late-night eating spells, I’ve gone to the bathroom and made myself throw up.\n\nIt’s usually after I’ve eaten way more than I meant to, and then I start thinking about it sitting in me, like all that food is going to immediately show on my body or make my clothes tighter or something. I know that probably sounds… I don’t know, not completely logical, but in the moment I get scared and kind of panicky about it.\n\nSo I’ve done that to try to undo it, I guess. Or make it feel like it didn’t count as much. And afterward I don’t exactly feel proud or relieved for very long. Mostly I feel ashamed that I did that too.\n\nDoctor: How often have you made yourself throw up after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: For about the last three months, I’d say it’s been… usually once a week. Like, there’s typically at least one night each week after class where I end up overeating like that, and then afterward I make myself throw up.\n\nIt’s not like every single day or after every meal or anything like that, and I think part of me wants to make it sound less serious because of that. But if I’m being honest, it has been pretty regular. Usually that one night a week where it happens, and it’s been going on for a few months now.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up afterward?\n\nPatient: It’s hard for me to put an exact number on the ones where I don’t throw up afterward, because I don’t really keep track in a neat way. But it does happen sometimes after my evening class.\n\nWhat usually happens is I’ll leave class feeling like I’ve been holding myself together all day, and then I’ll stop for food — like a full fast-food meal, not just a snack — and then I might get candy from a vending machine, and when I get home I’ll still keep going with whatever snacks are there. It feels almost like I’m making up for the whole day, like I didn’t eat “normally” earlier or I was trying to be careful, and then once I start, it just snowballs.\n\nSometimes after that I do make myself throw up, like I said, but other times I just… don’t. I sit with it, feel awful and overly full and embarrassed, and tell myself I shouldn’t do it again. But the eating a lot after class part is really the main pattern that brought me in.\n\nDoctor: How many times a month would you say you have these episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up afterward?\n\nPatient: I’d say maybe a few times a month… probably around two or three times where I eat a lot after class and don’t make myself throw up afterward.\n\nIt’s that pattern where I leave my evening class and I feel like I’ve been trying to be “good” all day, or holding off on eating much, and then I stop and get a full fast-food meal. And then sometimes it’s like that doesn’t end it — I’ll get candy from a vending machine, and when I get home I’ll start in on snacks there too. It feels like I’m making up for the whole day all at once, if that makes sense.\n\nSome of those times I do end up throwing up afterward, but the ones where I don’t… maybe two or three in a month. I’m not tracking it on a calendar, so I’m a little embarrassed guessing, but that sounds about right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after these episodes of eating are over, rather than just embarrassed or frustrated?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to separate those, honestly. I mean… I definitely feel bad afterward, but whether I’d call it guilt versus embarrassment or frustration, I don’t know.\n\nA lot of the time it’s more like this heavy feeling of, “Why did I do that again?” and I feel kind of ashamed sitting there thinking about it. But I don’t always have a clear thought like I did something morally wrong, if that’s what you mean by guilty. It’s more tangled than that — embarrassed, annoyed at myself, disappointed, and wanting to pretend it didn’t happen.\n\nSo… yes, there’s some guilt mixed in maybe, but I’m not sure I can cleanly say it’s guilt instead of the other feelings.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you have episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up afterward?\n\nPatient: In a week, I guess it’s not every week in the exact same way. If I’m trying to average it out, the times where I eat a lot after class and don’t make myself throw up afterward are maybe… less than once a week? Like some weeks it doesn’t happen, and then another week it might happen once.\n\nBut the pattern is pretty specific when it does happen. After my evening class, I’ll feel like I’ve been holding back all day, and then I stop and get a full fast-food meal, and sometimes I’ll add candy from a vending machine, and then when I get home I still keep eating snacks. It feels like I’m trying to make up for the whole day all at once.\n\nSo if you want it in “per week” terms, I’d probably say around once a week at most, and some weeks not at all.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes where you eat a lot and then make yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: For the ones where I do make myself throw up… that’s more regular, unfortunately. I’d say for about the last three months, there’s usually been at least one night each week after class where I overeat like that and then make myself throw up afterward.\n\nIt’s not like I’m writing it down, so I’m trying not to overstate it, but when I think back week by week, that’s about right — usually once a week, sometimes it feels like that one night after class where it happens. I’ll eat more than I meant to, and then afterward I panic or feel awful about it, and I end up making myself throw up.\n\nDoctor: How many episodes of making yourself throw up after eating have you had in the past month?\n\nPatient: In the past month… I think it’s been a few times. I’m trying to count it without making it sound more organized than it is, because it’s not like I mark it down or anything.\n\nIt happens after those late-night eating spells, especially when I feel like I’ve eaten way too much and then I start worrying that all that food is going to show on me somehow. A few times I’ve gone into the bathroom afterward and made myself throw up. It’s embarrassing to say out loud, but yes — in the past month, I’d say a few episodes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have more episodes of eating a lot after class where you don't make yourself throw up, or more episodes where you do make yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: More of the ones where I do make myself throw up, I think. I’ll go in thinking I’m just going to grab something quick because I haven’t really eaten much or I feel like I’m catching up from the day, and then it turns into more than I meant.\n\nLike, I might stop for a full fast-food meal, and then I’ll get candy from a vending machine, and then even after that, when I get home, I’ll end up eating more snacks. It feels almost like I’m making up for the whole day, like I didn’t eat “normally” earlier or I was trying to be careful, and then once I start, it just snowballs.\n\nSometimes after that I do make myself throw up, like I said, but other times I just… don’t. I sit with it, feel awful and overly full and embarrassed, and tell myself I shouldn’t do it again. If I’m trying to average it out, the times where I eat a lot after class and don’t make myself throw up afterward are maybe… less than once a week? Like some weeks it doesn’t happen, and then another week it might happen once.\n\nBut the pattern is pretty specific when it does happen. I’d say for about the last three months, there’s usually been at least one night each week after class where I overeat like that and then make myself throw up afterward.\n\nIt’s not like I’m writing it down, so I’m trying not to overstate it, but when I think back week by week, that’s about right — usually once a week, sometimes it feels like that one night after class where it happens. I’ll eat more than I meant to, and then afterward I panic or feel awful about it, and I end up making myself throw up.\n\nDoctor: How many episodes of making yourself throw up after eating have you had in the past month?\n\nPatient: In the past month… I think it’s been a few times. I haven’t noticed anything dramatic enough that I could point to it for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for these episodes of eating by doing something to counteract the calories you consumed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I don’t really like saying that out loud, but yes. Not every time, and I wouldn’t say it’s something I’ve done constantly, but a few times after those late-night eating spells, I’ve gone to the bathroom and made myself throw up.\n\nIt’s usually after I’ve eaten way more than I meant to, and then I start thinking about it sitting in me, like all that food is going to immediately show on my body or make my clothes tighter or something. I know that probably sounds… I don’t know, not completely logical, but in the moment I get scared and kind of panicky about it.\n\nSo I’ve done that to try to undo it, I guess. Or make it feel like it didn’t count as much. And afterward I don’t exactly feel proud or relieved for very long. Mostly I feel ashamed that I did that too.\n\nDoctor: How often have you made yourself throw up after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: For about the last three months, I’d say it’s been… usually once a week. Like, there’s typically at least one night each week after class where I end up overeating like that, and then afterward I make myself throw up.\n\nIt’s not like every single day or after every meal or anything like that, and I think part of me wants to make it sound less serious because of that. But if I’m being honest, it has been pretty regular. Usually that one night a week where it happens, and it’s been going on for a few months now.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up afterward?\n\nPatient: It’s hard for me to put an exact number on the ones where I don’t throw up afterward, because I don’t really keep track in a neat way. But it does happen sometimes after my evening class.\n\nWhat usually happens is I’ll leave class feeling like I’ve been holding myself together all day, and then I’ll stop for food — like a full fast-food meal, not just a snack — and then I might get candy from a vending machine, and when I get home I’ll still keep going with whatever snacks are there. It feels almost like I’m making up for the whole day, like I didn’t eat “normally” earlier or I was trying to be careful, and then once I start, it just snowballs.\n\nSometimes after that I do make myself throw up, like I said, but other times I just… don’t. I sit with it, feel awful and overly full and embarrassed, and tell myself I shouldn’t do it again. But the eating a lot after class part is really the main pattern that brought me in.\n\nDoctor: How many times a month would you say you have these episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up afterward?\n\nPatient: I’d say maybe a few times a month… probably around two or three times where I eat a lot after class and don’t make myself throw up afterward.\n\nIt’s that pattern where I leave my evening class and I feel like I’ve been trying to be “good” all day, or holding off on eating much, and then I stop and get a full fast-food meal. And then sometimes it’s like that doesn’t end it — I’ll get candy from a vending machine, and when I get home I’ll start in on snacks there too. It feels like I’m making up for the whole day all at once, if that makes sense.\n\nSome of those times I do end up throwing up afterward, but the ones where I don’t… maybe two or three in a month. I’m not tracking it on a calendar, so I’m a little embarrassed guessing, but that sounds about right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after these episodes of eating are over, rather than just embarrassed or frustrated?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to separate those, honestly. I mean… I definitely feel bad afterward, but whether I’d call it guilt versus embarrassment or frustration, I don’t know.\n\nA lot of the time it’s more like this heavy feeling of, “Why did I do that again?” and I feel kind of ashamed sitting there thinking about it. But I don’t always have a clear thought like I did something morally wrong, if that’s what you mean by guilty. It’s more tangled than that — embarrassed, annoyed at myself, disappointed, and wanting to pretend it didn’t happen.\n\nSo… yes, there’s some guilt mixed in maybe, but I’m not sure I can cleanly say it’s guilt instead of the other feelings.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you have episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up afterward?\n\nPatient: In a week, I guess it’s not every week in the exact same way. If I’m trying to average it out, the times where I eat a lot after class and don’t make myself throw up afterward are maybe… less than once a week? Like some weeks it doesn’t happen, and then another week it might happen once.\n\nBut the pattern is pretty specific when it does happen. After my evening class, I’ll feel like I’ve been holding back all day, and then I stop and get a full fast-food meal, and sometimes I’ll add candy from a vending machine, and then when I get home I still keep eating snacks. It feels like I’m trying to make up for the whole day all at once.\n\nSo if you want it in “per week” terms, I’d probably say around once a week at most, and some weeks not at all.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes where you eat a lot and then make yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: For the ones where I do make myself throw up… that’s more regular, unfortunately. I’d say for about the last three months, there’s usually been at least one night each week after class where I overeat like that and then make myself throw up afterward.\n\nIt’s not like I’m writing it down, so I’m trying not to overstate it, but when I think back week by week, that’s about right — usually once a week, sometimes it feels like that one night after class where it happens. I’ll eat more than I meant to, and then afterward I panic or feel awful about it, and I end up making myself throw up.\n\nDoctor: How many episodes of making yourself throw up after eating have you had in the past month?\n\nPatient: In the past month… I think it’s been a few times. I’m trying to count it without making it sound more organized than it is, because it’s not like I mark it down or anything.\n\nIt happens after those late-night eating spells, especially when I feel like I’ve eaten way too much and then I start worrying that all that food is going to show on me somehow. A few times I’ve gone into the bathroom afterward and made myself throw up. It’s embarrassing to say out loud, but yes — in the past month, I’d say a few episodes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have more episodes of eating a lot after class where you don't make yourself throw up, or more episodes where you do make yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: More of the ones where I do make myself throw up, I think. I mean, there are times after class where I eat too much and just… go to bed feeling terrible about it, but the ones that stick out are the nights where I get scared afterward.\n\nA few times after those late-night eating spells, I’ve gone to the bathroom and made myself throw up because I start thinking all that food is going to show on me somehow. It’s not every single time I overeat, but if you’re asking which happens more, I’d say the throwing up afterward has happened more than the times I just leave it alone.\n\nDoctor: On average, how many episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up do you have in a typical month?\n\nPatient: Maybe… a couple? It’s hard for me to separate it cleanly, because the whole thing usually starts the same way after my evening class. I’ll stop for food, and it’s not just like a normal snack — I’ll get a full fast-food meal, then candy from the vending machine, and then when I get home I’ll still keep eating, like chips or whatever snacks are around.\n\nThe times where I eat that much but don’t make myself throw up… I’d say maybe one or two times in a typical month. Most of the time, if it gets to that point where I’ve eaten like I’m making up for the whole day, I end up really upset afterward. But yes, there are some nights where I just go home, eat all that, feel awful, and don’t do anything after.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any episodes where you eat a lot after class and then engage in excessive exercise to compensate?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you mean, I don’t think.\n\nLike, I don’t usually go exercise afterward to “make up for it.” I might feel really guilty and restless after eating like that, and I’ll think things like, “I should do something,” but I don’t really have a pattern where I go running or work out for hours after class because I ate too much.\n\nI’m not much of an exercise person to begin with, honestly, and after those nights I’m usually more exhausted and ashamed than motivated. So if you mean specifically eating a lot after class and then doing a bunch of exercise to compensate, no, I haven’t really noticed that happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you had these episodes of eating a lot and then making yourself throw up at least twice a week for the past three months?\n\nPatient: Not twice a week, no… I don’t think it’s been that often. It’s more like, for about the last three months, there’s usually been at least one night each week after class where it happens — where I eat way too much and then end up making myself throw up afterward.\n\nSome weeks I guess it might feel like more because I’m thinking about it a lot, or because the whole night kind of stays with me, but if I’m being accurate, I wouldn’t say it’s consistently twice every week. The pattern I can say is pretty steady is once a week, most weeks, for these past few months.\n\nDoctor: Have you had these episodes of eating a lot and then making yourself throw up at least once a week for the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yes… once a week, yes. That’s probably the most accurate way to put it. For about the last three months, there’s usually been at least one night each week after class where I eat too much — like way past what I meant to — and then afterward I make myself throw up.\n\nIt’s not every single day or anything like that, and I know I already said it’s not really twice a week most of the time. But once a week… yeah, that has been happening pretty regularly. It’s usually tied to those class nights, so it kind of stands out in my mind.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

Yes… I think if we’re counting back honestly, it’s been about five months altogether. It started around when I began the evening Certified Bookkeeper classes.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.2, F50.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I guess what finally got me to come in is this thing that’s been happening with eating, mostly after my evening class. I’ll go in thinking I’m just going to grab something quick because I haven’t really eaten much or I feel like I’m catching up from the day, and then it turns into more than I meant.\n\nLike, I might stop for a full fast-food meal, and then I’ll get candy from a vending machine, and then even after that, when I get home, I’ll end up eating more snacks. It feels like I’m making up for the whole day all at once, if that makes sense. It’s not every second of my life, but it’s enough that I’m worried about it and embarrassed, and I thought maybe I should talk to someone before it gets worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these episodes after your evening class?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s the part that scares me a little, honestly. It doesn’t feel like normal snacking once it starts. I’ll tell myself, “Okay, just have a little and then put it away,” or “Close the bag and go to bed, you’re done,” and I’ll even sort of mean it in that moment.\n\nBut then my hand just keeps going back in. Like I’m watching myself do it and arguing with myself at the same time. I’ll think, “Why are you still eating this?” and then I’m still reaching for more anyway. It keeps going until I feel embarrassed and kind of sick of myself, like I should’ve been able to stop and I didn’t. So… no, I guess I don’t feel very in control during those times.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after these episodes of eating are over?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I don’t know if “guilty” is exactly the word every time. Sometimes I mostly feel embarrassed, like I don’t want anyone to know how much I ate or how fast it happened, and I’ll kind of replay it in my head afterward.\n\nThere’s definitely this feeling of, “Why did I do that again?” or “I should have had more self-control,” but I don’t know if that counts as guilt or just being frustrated with myself. It’s not like I’ve sat there and put a name to it, if that makes sense. I just know I don’t feel good about it afterward.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over time due to these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I mean, I notice little ups and downs, like my pants feeling tighter some weeks and then maybe normal again, but I haven’t been tracking it in any serious way. I don’t weigh myself consistently, partly because I don’t really want to know, if I’m being honest.\n\nSo I can’t say there’s been some big, clear change where I know, “Oh, this is because of those nights after class.” It’s more that I worry it might be affecting me, and then I try not to think about it too much. I haven’t noticed anything dramatic enough that I could point to it for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for these episodes of eating by doing something to counteract the calories you consumed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I don’t really like saying that out loud, but yes. Not every time, and I wouldn’t say it’s something I’ve done constantly, but a few times after those late-night eating spells, I’ve gone to the bathroom and made myself throw up.\n\nIt’s usually after I’ve eaten way more than I meant to, and then I start thinking about it sitting in me, like all that food is going to immediately show on my body or make my clothes tighter or something. I know that probably sounds… I don’t know, not completely logical, but in the moment I get scared and kind of panicky about it.\n\nSo I’ve done that to try to undo it, I guess. Or make it feel like it didn’t count as much. And afterward I don’t exactly feel proud or relieved for very long. Mostly I feel ashamed that I did that too.\n\nDoctor: How often have you made yourself throw up after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: For about the last three months, I’d say it’s been… usually once a week. Like, there’s typically at least one night each week after class where I end up overeating like that, and then afterward I make myself throw up.\n\nIt’s not like every single day or after every meal or anything like that, and I think part of me wants to make it sound less serious because of that. But if I’m being honest, it has been pretty regular. Usually that one night a week where it happens, and it’s been going on for a few months now.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up afterward?\n\nPatient: It’s hard for me to put an exact number on the ones where I don’t throw up afterward, because I don’t really keep track in a neat way. But it does happen sometimes after my evening class.\n\nWhat usually happens is I’ll leave class feeling like I’ve been holding myself together all day, and then I’ll stop for food — like a full fast-food meal, not just a snack — and then I might get candy from a vending machine, and when I get home I’ll still keep going with whatever snacks are there. It feels almost like I’m making up for the whole day, like I didn’t eat “normally” earlier or I was trying to be careful, and then once I start, it just snowballs.\n\nSometimes after that I do make myself throw up, like I said, but other times I just… don’t. I sit with it, feel awful and overly full and embarrassed, and tell myself I shouldn’t do it again. But the eating a lot after class part is really the main pattern that brought me in.\n\nDoctor: How many times a month would you say you have these episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up afterward?\n\nPatient: I’d say maybe a few times a month… probably around two or three times where I eat a lot after class and don’t make myself throw up afterward.\n\nIt’s that pattern where I leave my evening class and I feel like I’ve been trying to be “good” all day, or holding off on eating much, and then I stop and get a full fast-food meal. And then sometimes it’s like that doesn’t end it — I’ll get candy from a vending machine, and when I get home I’ll start in on snacks there too. It feels like I’m making up for the whole day all at once, if that makes sense.\n\nSome of those times I do end up throwing up afterward, but the ones where I don’t… maybe two or three in a month. I’m not tracking it on a calendar, so I’m a little embarrassed guessing, but that sounds about right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after these episodes of eating are over, rather than just embarrassed or frustrated?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to separate those, honestly. I mean… I definitely feel bad afterward, but whether I’d call it guilt versus embarrassment or frustration, I don’t know.\n\nA lot of the time it’s more like this heavy feeling of, “Why did I do that again?” and I feel kind of ashamed sitting there thinking about it. But I don’t always have a clear thought like I did something morally wrong, if that’s what you mean by guilty. It’s more tangled than that — embarrassed, annoyed at myself, disappointed, and wanting to pretend it didn’t happen.\n\nSo… yes, there’s some guilt mixed in maybe, but I’m not sure I can cleanly say it’s guilt instead of the other feelings.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you have episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up afterward?\n\nPatient: In a week, I guess it’s not every week in the exact same way. If I’m trying to average it out, the times where I eat a lot after class and don’t make myself throw up afterward are maybe… less than once a week? Like some weeks it doesn’t happen, and then another week it might happen once.\n\nBut the pattern is pretty specific when it does happen. After my evening class, I’ll feel like I’ve been holding back all day, and then I stop and get a full fast-food meal, and sometimes I’ll add candy from a vending machine, and then when I get home I still keep eating snacks. It feels like I’m trying to make up for the whole day all at once.\n\nSo if you want it in “per week” terms, I’d probably say around once a week at most, and some weeks not at all.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes where you eat a lot and then make yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: For the ones where I do make myself throw up… that’s more regular, unfortunately. I’d say for about the last three months, there’s usually been at least one night each week after class where I overeat like that and then make myself throw up afterward.\n\nIt’s not like I’m writing it down, so I’m trying not to overstate it, but when I think back week by week, that’s about right — usually once a week, sometimes it feels like that one night after class where it happens. I’ll eat more than I meant to, and then afterward I panic or feel awful about it, and I end up making myself throw up.\n\nDoctor: How many episodes of making yourself throw up after eating have you had in the past month?\n\nPatient: In the past month… I think it’s been a few times. I’m trying to count it without making it sound more organized than it is, because it’s not like I mark it down or anything.\n\nIt happens after those late-night eating spells, especially when I feel like I’ve eaten way too much and then I start worrying that all that food is going to show on me somehow. A few times I’ve gone into the bathroom afterward and made myself throw up. It’s embarrassing to say out loud, but yes — in the past month, I’d say a few episodes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have more episodes of eating a lot after class where you don't make yourself throw up, or more episodes where you do make yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: More of the ones where I do make myself throw up, I think. I mean, there are times after class where I eat too much and just… go to bed feeling terrible about it, but the ones that stick out are the nights where I get scared afterward.\n\nA few times after those late-night eating spells, I’ve gone to the bathroom and made myself throw up because I start thinking all that food is going to show on me somehow. It’s not every single time I overeat, but if you’re asking which happens more, I’d say the throwing up afterward has happened more than the times I just leave it alone.\n\nDoctor: On average, how many episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up do you have in a typical month?\n\nPatient: Maybe… a couple? It’s hard for me to separate it cleanly, because the whole thing usually starts the same way after my evening class. I’ll stop for food, and it’s not just like a normal snack — I’ll get a full fast-food meal, then candy from the vending machine, and then when I get home I’ll still keep eating, like chips or whatever snacks are around.\n\nThe times where I eat that much but don’t make myself throw up… I’d say maybe one or two times in a typical month. Most of the time, if it gets to that point where I’ve eaten like I’m making up for the whole day, I end up really upset afterward. But yes, there are some nights where I just go home, eat all that, feel awful, and don’t do anything after.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any episodes where you eat a lot after class and then engage in excessive exercise to compensate?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you mean, I don’t think.\n\nLike, I don’t usually go exercise afterward to “make up for it.” I might feel really guilty and restless after eating like that, and I’ll think things like, “I should do something,” but I don’t really have a pattern where I go running or work out for hours after class because I ate too much.\n\nI’m not much of an exercise person to begin with, honestly, and after those nights I’m usually more exhausted and ashamed than motivated. So if you mean specifically eating a lot after class and then doing a bunch of exercise to compensate, no, I haven’t really noticed that happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you had these episodes of eating a lot and then making yourself throw up at least twice a week for the past three months?\n\nPatient: Not twice a week, no… I don’t think it’s been that often. It’s more like, for about the last three months, there’s usually been at least one night each week after class where it happens — where I eat way too much and then end up making myself throw up afterward.\n\nSome weeks I guess it might feel like more because I’m thinking about it a lot, or because the whole night kind of stays with me, but if I’m being accurate, I wouldn’t say it’s consistently twice every week. The pattern I can say is pretty steady is once a week, most weeks, for these past few months.\n\nDoctor: Have you had these episodes of eating a lot and then making yourself throw up at least once a week for the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yes… once a week, yes.
