It’s more tangled than that — embarrassed, annoyed at myself, disappointed, and wanting to pretend it didn’t happen.\n\nSo… yes, there’s some guilt mixed in maybe, but I’m not sure I can cleanly say it’s guilt instead of the other feelings.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you have episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up afterward?\n\nPatient: In a week, I guess it’s not every week in the exact same way. If I’m trying to average it out, the times where I eat a lot after class and don’t make myself throw up afterward are maybe… less than once a week? Like some weeks it doesn’t happen, and then another week it might happen once.\n\nBut the pattern is pretty specific when it does happen. After my evening class, I’ll feel like I’ve been holding back all day, and then I stop and get a full fast-food meal, and sometimes I’ll add candy from a vending machine, and then when I get home I still keep eating snacks. It feels like I’m trying to make up for the whole day all at once.\n\nSo if you want it in “per week” terms, I’d probably say around once a week at most, and some weeks not at all.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes where you eat a lot and then make yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: For the ones where I do make myself throw up… that’s more regular, unfortunately. I’d say for about the last three months, there’s usually been at least one night each week after class where I overeat like that and then make myself throw up afterward.\n\nIt’s not like I’m writing it down, so I’m trying not to overstate it, but when I think back week by week, that’s about right — usually once a week, sometimes it feels like that one night after class where it happens. I’ll eat more than I meant to, and then afterward I panic or feel awful about it, and I end up making myself throw up.\n\nDoctor: How many episodes of making yourself throw up after eating have you had in the past month?\n\nPatient: In the past month… I think it’s been a few times. I’m trying to count it without making it sound more organized than it is, because it’s not like I mark it down or anything.\n\nIt happens after those late-night eating spells, especially when I feel like I’ve eaten way too much and then I start worrying that all that food is going to show on me somehow. A few times I’ve gone into the bathroom afterward and made myself throw up. It’s embarrassing to say out loud, but yes — in the past month, I’d say a few episodes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have more episodes of eating a lot after class where you don't make yourself throw up, or more episodes where you do make yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: More of the ones where I do make myself throw up, I think. I mean, there are times after class where I eat too much and just… go to bed feeling terrible about it, but the ones that stick out are the nights where I get scared afterward.\n\nA few times after those late-night eating spells, I’ve gone to the bathroom and made myself throw up because I start thinking all that food is going to show on me somehow. It’s not every single time I overeat, but if you’re asking which happens more, I’d say the throwing up afterward has happened more than the times I just leave it alone.\n\nDoctor: On average, how many episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up do you have in a typical month?\n\nPatient: Maybe… a couple? It’s hard for me to separate it cleanly, because the whole thing usually starts the same way after my evening class. I’ll stop for food, and it’s not just like a normal snack — I’ll get a full fast-food meal, then candy from the vending machine, and then when I get home I’ll still keep eating, like chips or whatever snacks are around.\n\nThe times where I eat that much but don’t make myself throw up… I’d say maybe one or two times in a typical month. Most of the time, if it gets to that point where I’ve eaten like I’m making up for the whole day, I end up really upset afterward. But yes, there are some nights where I just go home, eat all that, feel awful, and don’t do anything after.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any episodes where you eat a lot after class and then engage in excessive exercise to compensate?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you mean, I don’t think.\n\nLike, I don’t usually go exercise afterward to “make up for it.” I might feel really guilty and restless after eating like that, and I’ll think things like, “I should do something,” but I don’t really have a pattern where I go running or work out for hours after class because I ate too much.\n\nI’m not much of an exercise person to begin with, honestly, and after those nights I’m usually more exhausted and ashamed than motivated. So if you mean specifically eating a lot after class and then doing a bunch of exercise to compensate, no, I haven’t really noticed that happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you had these episodes of eating a lot and then making yourself throw up at least twice a week for the past three months?\n\nPatient: Not twice a week, no… I don’t think it’s been that often. It’s more like, for about the last three months, there’s usually been at least one night each week after class where it happens — where I eat way too much and then end up making myself throw up afterward.\n\nSome weeks I guess it might feel like more because I’m thinking about it a lot, or because the whole night kind of stays with me, but if I’m being accurate, I wouldn’t say it’s consistently twice every week. The pattern I can say is pretty steady is once a week, most weeks, for these past few months.\n\nDoctor: Have you had these episodes of eating a lot and then making yourself throw up at least once a week for the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yes… once a week, yes. That’s probably the most accurate way to put it. For about the last three months, there’s usually been at least one night each week after class where I eat too much — like way past what I meant to — and then afterward I make myself throw up.\n\nIt’s not every single day or anything like that, and I know I already said it’s not really twice a week most of the time. But once a week… yeah, that has been happening pretty regularly. It’s usually tied to those class nights, so it kind of stands out in my mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you had these episodes of eating a lot and then making yourself throw up at least once a week for the past two months, in addition to the past three months you mentioned?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think if we’re counting back honestly, it’s been about five months altogether. It started around when I began the evening Certified Bookkeeper classes.\n\nAt first I didn’t really think of it as a “pattern,” because it was just tied to those class nights and I kept telling myself it was stress or being tired after sitting there all evening. But looking back, yes, it’s not only the last three months. A few times I’ve gone into the bathroom afterward and made myself throw up. I mean, there are times after class where I eat too much and just… go to bed feeling terrible about it, but the ones that stick out are the nights where I get scared afterward.\n\nA few times after those late-night eating spells, I’ve gone to the bathroom and made myself throw up because I start thinking all that food is going to show on me somehow. It’s not every single time I overeat, but if you’re asking which happens more, I’d say the throwing up afterward has happened more than the times I just leave it alone.\n\nDoctor: On average, how many episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up do you have in a typical month?\n\nPatient: Maybe… a couple? It’s hard for me to separate it cleanly, because the whole thing usually starts the same way after my evening class. I’ll stop for food, and it’s not just like a normal snack — I’ll get a full fast-food meal, then candy from the vending machine, and then when I get home I’ll still keep eating, like chips or whatever snacks are around.\n\nThe times where I eat that much but don’t make myself throw up… I’d say maybe one or two times in a typical month. Most of the time, if it gets to that point where I’ve eaten like I’m making up for the whole day, I end up really upset afterward. But yes, there are some nights where I just go home, eat all that, feel awful, and don’t do anything after.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any episodes where you eat a lot after class and then engage in excessive exercise to compensate?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you mean, I don’t think.\n\nLike, I don’t usually go exercise afterward to “make up for it.” I might feel really guilty and restless after eating like that, and I’ll think things like, “I should do something,” but I don’t really have a pattern where I go running or work out for hours after class because I ate too much.\n\nI’m not much of an exercise person to begin with, honestly, and after those nights I’m usually more exhausted and ashamed than motivated. So if you mean specifically eating a lot after class and then doing a bunch of exercise to compensate, no, I haven’t really noticed that happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you had these episodes of eating a lot and then making yourself throw up at least twice a week for the past three months?\n\nPatient: Not twice a week, no… I don’t think it’s been that often. It’s more like, for about the last three months, there’s usually been at least one night each week after class where it happens — where I eat way too much and then end up making myself throw up afterward.\n\nSome weeks I guess it might feel like more because I’m thinking about it a lot, or because the whole night kind of stays with me, but if I’m being accurate, I wouldn’t say it’s consistently twice every week. The pattern I can say is pretty steady is once a week, most weeks, for these past few months.\n\nDoctor: Have you had these episodes of eating a lot and then making yourself throw up at least once a week for the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yes… once a week, yes. For about the last three months, there’s usually been at least one night each week after class where I eat too much — like way past what I meant to — and then afterward I make myself throw up.\n\nIt’s not every single day or anything like that, and I know I already said it’s not really twice a week most of the time. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.   },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… definitely in the past month. I mean, if anything, the past month is part of the same pattern I’ve been trying to describe. There’s usually been at least one night each week after class where I eat way more than I planned — more than feels normal for me — and then afterward I make myself throw up.

It’s embarrassing to say it that plainly, but yes, over this last month that has been happening at least weekly.

I’ll go in thinking I’m just going to grab something quick because I haven’t really eaten much or I feel like I’m catching up from the day, and then it turns into more than I meant.\n\nLike, I might stop for a full fast-food meal, and then I’ll get candy from a vending machine, and then even after that, when I get home, I’ll end up eating more snacks. It feels like I’m making up for the whole day all at once, if that makes sense. It’s not every second of my life, but it’s enough that I’m worried about it and embarrassed, and I thought maybe I should talk to someone before it gets worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these episodes after your evening class?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s the part that scares me a little, honestly. It doesn’t feel like normal snacking once it starts. I don’t weigh myself consistently, partly because I don’t really want to know, if I’m being honest.\n\nSo I can’t say there’s been some big, clear change where I know, “Oh, this is because of those nights after class.” It’s more that I worry it might be affecting me, and then I try not to think about it too much. I sit with it, feel awful and overly full and embarrassed, and tell myself I shouldn’t do it again. But the eating a lot after class part is really the main pattern that brought me in.\n\nDoctor: How many times a month would you say you have these episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up afterward?\n\nPatient: I’d say maybe a few times a month… probably around two or three times where I eat a lot after class and don’t make myself throw up afterward.\n\nIt’s that pattern where I leave my evening class and I feel like I’ve been trying to be “good” all day, or holding off on eating much, and then I stop and get a full fast-food meal. A few times I’ve gone into the bathroom afterward and made myself throw up. It’s embarrassing to say out loud, but yes — in the past month, I’d say a few episodes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have more episodes of eating a lot after class where you don't make yourself throw up, or more episodes where you do make yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: More of the ones where I do make myself throw up, I think. I’d say for about the last three months, there’s usually been at least one night each week after class where I overeat like that and then make myself throw up afterward.\n\nIt’s not like I’m writing it down, so I’m trying not to overstate it, but when I think back week by week, that’s about right — usually once a week, sometimes it feels like that one night after class where it happens. I’ll eat more than I meant to, and then afterward I panic or feel awful about it, and I end up making myself throw up.\n\nDoctor: How many episodes of making yourself throw up after eating have you had in the past month?\n\nPatient: In the past month… I think it’s been a few times. I’m trying to count it without making it sound more organized than it is, because it’s not like I mark it down or anything.\n\nIt happens after those late-night eating spells, especially when I feel like I’ve eaten way too much and then I start worrying that all that food is going to show on me somehow. A few times I’ve gone into the bathroom afterward and made myself throw up. It’s embarrassing to say out loud, but yes — in the past month, I’d say a few episodes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have more episodes of eating a lot after class where you don't make yourself throw up, or more episodes where you do make yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: More of the ones where I do make myself throw up, I think. I mean, there are times after class where I eat too much and just… go to bed feeling terrible about it, but the ones that stick out are the nights where I get scared afterward.\n\nA few times after those late-night eating spells, I’ve gone to the bathroom and made myself throw up because I start thinking all that food is going to show on me somehow. It’s not every single time I overeat, but if you’re asking which happens more, I’d say the throwing up afterward has happened more than the times I just leave it alone.\n\nDoctor: On average, how many episodes of eating a lot after class without making yourself throw up do you have in a typical month?\n\nPatient: Maybe… a couple? It’s hard for me to separate it cleanly, because the whole thing usually starts the same way after my evening class. I’ll stop for food, and it’s not just like a normal snack — I’ll get a full fast-food meal, then candy from the vending machine, and then when I get home I’ll still keep eating, like chips or whatever snacks are around.\n\nThe times where I eat that much but don’t make myself throw up… I’d say maybe one or two times in a typical month. Most of the time, if it gets to that point where I’ve eaten like I’m making up for the whole day, I end up really upset afterward. But yes, there are some nights where I just go home, eat all that, feel awful, and don’t do anything after.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any episodes where you eat a lot after class and then engage in excessive exercise to compensate?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you mean, I don’t think.\n\nLike, I don’t usually go exercise afterward to “make up for it.” I might feel really guilty and restless after eating like that, and I’ll think things like, “I should do something,” but I don’t really have a pattern where I go running or work out for hours after class because I ate too much.\n\nI’m not much of an exercise person to begin with, honestly, and after those nights I’m usually more exhausted and ashamed than motivated. So if you mean specifically eating a lot after class and then doing a bunch of exercise to compensate, no, I haven’t really noticed that happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you had these episodes of eating a lot and then making yourself throw up at least twice a week for the past three months?\n\nPatient: Not twice a week, no… I don’t think it’s been that often. It’s more like, for about the last three months, there’s usually been at least one night each week after class where it happens — where I eat way too much and then end up making myself throw up afterward.\n\nSome weeks I guess it might feel like more because I’m thinking about it a lot, or because the whole night kind of stays with me, but if I’m being accurate, I wouldn’t say it’s consistently twice every week. The pattern I can say is pretty steady is once a week, most weeks, for these past few months.\n\nDoctor: Have you had these episodes of eating a lot and then making yourself throw up at least once a week for the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yes… once a week, yes. That’s probably the most accurate way to put it. For about the last three months, there’s usually been at least one night each week after class where I eat too much — like way past what I meant to — and then afterward I make myself throw up.\n\nIt’s not every single day or anything like that, and I know I already said it’s not really twice a week most of the time. But once a week… yeah, that has been happening pretty regularly. It’s usually tied to those class nights, so it kind of stands out in my mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you had these episodes of eating a lot and then making yourself throw up at least once a week for the past two months, in addition to the past three months you mentioned?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think if we’re counting back honestly, it’s been about five months altogether. It started around when I began the evening Certified Bookkeeper classes.\n\nAt first I didn’t really think of it as a “pattern,” because it was just tied to those class nights and I kept telling myself it was stress or being tired after sitting there all evening. But looking back, yes, it’s not only the last three months. It was going on for about two months before that too, so about five months total since those classes started.\n\nDoctor: Have you had these episodes of eating a lot and then making yourself throw up at least once a week for the past six months?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I can honestly say six months. It’s been about five months, from when I started those evening Certified Bookkeeper classes.\n\nI know that’s close to six, and it feels like it’s been hanging over me for a long time, but if I’m trying to be accurate, I’d say it started around the beginning of those classes, not a full six months ago. So once a week for about five months, yes… but six months, I don’t think so.\n\nDoctor: Have you had these episodes of eating a lot and then making yourself throw up at least once a week for the past month?\n\nPatient: Yes… definitely in the past month. I mean, if anything, the past month is part of the same pattern I’ve been trying to describe. There’s usually been at least one night each week after class where I eat way more than I planned — more than feels normal for me — and then afterward I make myself throw up.\n\nIt’s embarrassing to say it that plainly, but yes, over this last month that has been happening at least weekly."
